The company is closely related to — and likely a subsidiary or DBA of — WashSense Inc. and Arsana Health. The contact listed on CHPL (Laura Ivanoski) uses a `washsense.com` email address and serves as VP of Finance & Operations at WashSense. The BroadStreet product portal is hosted at `broadstreet.arsanahealth.com`.\n     9→\n    10→**Corporate structure:** The overall entity appears to be **Arsana Health**, founded by Connor Dahlberg, headquartered at the Black River Innovation Campus in Springfield, Vermont. Arsana Health operates a \"Multi-Provider Integrated Platform\" with two main components:\n    11→1. **Arsana Health Portal / BroadStreet** — an EHR software platform\n    12→2. **WashSense** — smart handwashing/infection surveillance hardware devices\n    13→\n    14→The CORI Innovation Fund led a funding round for Arsana Health (announced ~June 2020), providing capital to expand in the post-acute care market. Thomas Dahlberg (a rural health physician) is listed as co-founder of WashSense. The company appears very early-stage with minimal market presence — no third-party reviews (G2, KLAS, Capterra) were found, the main website at broadstreetcare.com shows a \"Coming Soon\" placeholder for most content, and web searches return almost no customer references or independent coverage.\n    15→\n    16→## Product: BroadStreet\n    17→\n    18→CHPL ID: 11410 (15.05.05.3161.BRDS.01.00.1.231222)\n    19→\n    20→### What It Is\n    21→\n    22→BroadStreet Version 1 is an ONC-certified Electronic Health Record system specifically designed for post-acute care and community-based healthcare settings, including skilled nursing facilities and assisted living homes. It was certified on December 22, 2023.\n    23→\n    24→Per the mandatory disclosures page, BroadStreet was \"developed with the goal of creating a new way to experience, interact, and deliver care.\" The product's usability testing report (from SLI Compliance, December 2024) describes it as featuring \"a unique task architecture that enhances the way users capture, modify, and interact with patient health information.\"\n    25→\n    26→BroadStreet is part of the broader Arsana Health platform, which positions itself as combining medical records from multiple sources (post-acute care facilities, doctors' offices, labs, and pharmacies) into a unified interface. The Arsana platform also includes AI-powered analytics for health risk detection (fever, respiratory symptoms, antibiotic usage) and care coordination capabilities.\n    27→\n    28→### Users & Market\n    29→\n    30→**Target users:** Post-acute and community-based health providers (per the CHPL `sed_intended_user_description`). This means staff at skilled nursing facilities (SNFs), assisted living communities, and potentially other long-term care settings.\n    31→\n    32→**Market presence:** Essentially unknown. No customer count, no case studies, no third-party reviews were found. The vendor website is largely a placeholder. The company has fewer than 10 employees. This appears to be an early-stage product with very limited deployment.\n    33→\n    34→**Geography:** Based in Springfield, Vermont (at the Black River Innovation Campus, designated as a federal Opportunity Zone). Vermont rural health focus is suggested by the founder's background and CORI Innovation Fund investment.\n    35→\n    36→**No reviews found** on G2, KLAS, Capterra, or any other third-party review platform. No press coverage of customer deployments.\n    37→\n    38→### Modules & Functionality\n    39→\n    40→Based on the mandatory disclosures page and certified criteria, BroadStreet includes the following capabilities:\n    41→\n    42→**Clinical Documentation & Orders (from certified criteria):**\n    43→- CPOE for medications — 170.315(a)(1)\n    44→- CPOE for laboratory orders — 170.315(a)(2)\n    45→- CPOE for diagnostic imaging orders — 170.315(a)(3)\n    46→- Demographics capture — 170.315(a)(5)\n    47→- Family health history — 170.315(a)(12)\n    48→- Implantable device list — 170.315(a)(14)\n    49→- Social, psychological, and behavioral data capture — 170.315(a)(15)\n    50→\n    51→**Care Coordination & Interoperability:**\n    52→- Transitions of care (C-CDA) — 170.315(b)(1)\n    53→- Clinical information reconciliation (medications, problems, allergies) — 170.315(b)(2)\n    54→- EHI export — 170.315(b)(10)\n    55→- Care plan — 170.315(b)(11)\n    56→- Direct secure messaging via EMR Direct Interoperability Engine (requires annual subscription) — 170.315(h)(1)\n    57→\n    58→**Patient Access:**\n    59→- View, download, and transmit health information — 170.315(e)(1)\n    60→- Patient-generated health data — 170.315(e)(3)\n    61→\n    62→**Clinical Quality Measures (CQMs):**\n    63→- CMS2 v13: Screening for depression and follow-up plan\n    64→- CMS69 v12: Preventive care and screening: BMI screening and follow-up\n    65→- CMS138 v12: Preventive care and screening: tobacco use screening and cessation intervention\n    66→- CMS139 v12: Falls: screening for future fall risk\n    67→- CMS149 v12: Dementia: cognitive assessment\n    68→- CMS951 v2: Kidney health evaluation\n    69→\n    70→The CQM selections are strongly indicative of the post-acute/geriatric care focus — fall risk, dementia cognitive assessment, depression screening, and kidney health are core measures for skilled nursing and long-term care populations.\n    71→\n    72→**FHIR API Access:**\n    73→- Standardized API for patient and population services — 170.315(g)(7), (g)(9), (g)(10)\n    74→\n    75→**Security & Infrastructure:**\n    76→- Full suite of security criteria: (d)(1) through (d)(13), covering authentication, access control, audit logging, encryption, emergency access, and multi-factor authentication.\n    77→\n    78→**Usability testing (from SLI Compliance report):** Tasks tested included collecting, modifying, and reconciling outside records; working with demographics, implantable devices, labs, diagnostics, and medications.\n    79→\n    80→**Broader Arsana Platform capabilities** (from washsense.com/post-acute and press coverage, not specifically from BroadStreet certification docs):\n    81→- Infection surveillance with cross-contamination mapping\n    82→- Chronic care management\n    83→- Remote clinical evaluation of complex cases\n    84→- Advanced care planning\n    85→- Hospital diversion initiatives\n    86→- AI-powered health risk analytics (fever, respiratory symptoms, antibiotic usage patterns)\n    87→- Integration with WashSense smart handwashing devices for real-time hygiene compliance monitoring\n    88→\n    89→It is unclear how much of the broader Arsana platform is part of the certified BroadStreet product vs. separate products in the same ecosystem.\n    90→\n    91→### Data & Content\n    92→\n    93→**Data types confirmed by certification criteria:**\n    94→- Patient demographics\n    95→- Medication orders and medication lists\n    96→- Laboratory orders and results\n    97→- Diagnostic imaging orders\n    98→- Problem lists\n    99→- Allergy lists\n   100→- Family health history\n   101→- Implantable device records\n   102→- Social, psychological, and behavioral data (SDOH-adjacent)\n   103→- Care plans\n   104→- Clinical quality measure data (depression screening, BMI, tobacco use, fall risk, cognitive assessment, kidney health)\n   105→- C-CDA documents (for transitions of care)\n   106→- Patient-generated health data\n   107→- Audit logs and access records\n   108→\n   109→**Data types implied by Arsana platform marketing (but not confirmed as part of BroadStreet specifically):**\n   110→- Infection surveillance data\n   111→- Hand hygiene compliance data\n   112→- Cross-contamination mapping\n   113→- Records aggregated from external sources (doctors' offices, labs, pharmacies)\n   114→\n   115→**What's unclear / not mentioned:**\n   116→- **Billing and claims data:** Not mentioned anywhere. The certification criteria don't include billing-related items, and neither the vendor website nor any marketing materials mention billing functionality. The Arsana/CORI press release mentions the platform \"creates new billing opportunities\" but doesn't describe integrated billing/claims modules. Billing is likely handled by a separate system.\n   117→- **Scheduling:** Not mentioned.\n   118→- **Progress notes / clinical narrative documentation:** Not explicitly described, though likely present given the clinical charting implied by CPOE and CQM capabilities.\n   119→- **Nursing assessments (MDS, OASIS, etc.):** Not mentioned, despite the post-acute focus where MDS assessments are a core workflow for SNFs. This is a notable gap in available information.\n   120→- **eMAR (electronic medication administration record):** Not mentioned, though this would be expected for a post-acute EHR.\n   121→- **Pharmacy integration:** Not explicitly described.\n   122→- **Messaging/communication between staff:** Not mentioned.\n   123→\n   124→The overall picture is of an early-stage post-acute EHR with a solid set of ONC-certified clinical capabilities, but with very limited publicly available information about the full breadth of the product's functionality. The company is closely related to — and likely a subsidiary or DBA of — WashSense Inc. and Arsana Health. The contact listed on CHPL (Laura Ivanoski) uses a `washsense.com` email address and serves as VP of Finance & Operations at WashSense. The BroadStreet product portal is hosted at `broadstreet.arsanahealth.com`.\n\n**Corporate structure:** The overall entity appears to be **Arsana Health**, founded by Connor Dahlberg, headquartered at the Black River Innovation Campus in Springfield, Vermont. Arsana Health operates a \"Multi-Provider Integrated Platform\" with two main components:\n1. **Arsana Health Portal / BroadStreet** — an EHR software platform\n2. **WashSense** — smart handwashing/infection surveillance hardware devices\n\nThe CORI Innovation Fund led a funding round for Arsana Health (announced ~June 2020), providing capital to expand in the post-acute care market. Thomas Dahlberg (a rural health physician) is listed as co-founder of WashSense. The company appears very early-stage with minimal market presence — no third-party reviews (G2, KLAS, Capterra) were found, the main website at broadstreetcare.com shows a \"Coming Soon\" placeholder for most content, and web searches return almost no customer references or independent coverage.\n\n## Product: BroadStreet\n\nCHPL ID: 11410 (15.05.05.3161.BRDS.01.00.1.231222)\n\n### What It Is\n\nBroadStreet Version 1 is an ONC-certified Electronic Health Record system specifically designed for post-acute care and community-based healthcare settings, including skilled nursing facilities and assisted living homes. It was certified on December 22, 2023.\n\nPer the mandatory disclosures page, BroadStreet was \"developed with the goal of creating a new way to experience, interact, and deliver care.\" The product's usability testing report (from SLI Compliance, December 2024) describes it as featuring \"a unique task architecture that enhances the way users capture, modify, and interact with patient health information.\"\n\nBroadStreet is part of the broader Arsana Health platform, which positions itself as combining medical records from multiple sources (post-acute care facilities, doctors' offices, labs, and pharmacies) into a unified interface. The Arsana platform also includes AI-powered analytics for health risk detection (fever, respiratory symptoms, antibiotic usage) and care coordination capabilities.\n\n### Users & Market\n\n**Target users:** Post-acute and community-based health providers (per the CHPL `sed_intended_user_description`). This means staff at skilled nursing facilities (SNFs), assisted living communities, and potentially other long-term care settings.\n\n**Market presence:** Essentially unknown. No customer count, no case studies, no third-party reviews were found. The vendor website is largely a placeholder. The company has fewer than 10 employees. This appears to be an early-stage product with very limited deployment.\n\n**Geography:** Based in Springfield, Vermont (at the Black River Innovation Campus, designated as a federal Opportunity Zone). Vermont rural health focus is suggested by the founder's background and CORI Innovation Fund investment.\n\n**No reviews found** on G2, KLAS, Capterra, or any other third-party review platform. No press coverage of customer deployments.\n\n### Modules & Functionality\n\nBased on the mandatory disclosures page and certified criteria, BroadStreet includes the following capabilities:\n\n**Clinical Documentation & Orders (from certified criteria):**\n- CPOE for medications — 170.315(a)(1)\n- CPOE for laboratory orders — 170.315(a)(2)\n- CPOE for diagnostic imaging orders — 170.315(a)(3)\n- Demographics capture — 170.315(a)(5)\n- Family health history — 170.315(a)(12)\n- Implantable device list — 170.315(a)(14)\n- Social, psychological, and behavioral data capture — 170.315(a)(15)\n\n**Care Coordination & Interoperability:**\n- Transitions of care (C-CDA) — 170.315(b)(1)\n- Clinical information reconciliation (medications, problems, allergies) — 170.315(b)(2)\n- EHI export — 170.315(b)(10)\n- Care plan — 170.315(b)(11)\n- Direct secure messaging via EMR Direct Interoperability Engine (requires annual subscription) — 170.315(h)(1)\n\n**Patient Access:**\n- View, download, and transmit health information — 170.315(e)(1)\n- Patient-generated health data — 170.315(e)(3)\n\n**Clinical Quality Measures (CQMs):**\n- CMS2 v13: Screening for depression and follow-up plan\n- CMS69 v12: Preventive care and screening: BMI screening and follow-up\n- CMS138 v12: Preventive care and screening: tobacco use screening and cessation intervention\n- CMS139 v12: Falls: screening for future fall risk\n- CMS149 v12: Dementia: cognitive assessment\n- CMS951 v2: Kidney health evaluation\n\nThe CQM selections are strongly indicative of the post-acute/geriatric care focus — fall risk, dementia cognitive assessment, depression screening, and kidney health are core measures for skilled nursing and long-term care populations.\n\n**FHIR API Access:**\n- Standardized API for patient and population services — 170.315(g)(7), (g)(9), (g)(10)\n\n**Security & Infrastructure:**\n- Full suite of security criteria: (d)(1) through (d)(13), covering authentication, access control, audit logging, encryption, emergency access, and multi-factor authentication.\n\n**Usability testing (from SLI Compliance report):** Tasks tested included collecting, modifying, and reconciling outside records; working with demographics, implantable devices, labs, diagnostics, and medications.\n\n**Broader Arsana Platform capabilities** (from washsense.com/post-acute and press coverage, not specifically from BroadStreet certification docs):\n- Infection surveillance with cross-contamination mapping\n- Chronic care management\n- Remote clinical evaluation of complex cases\n- Advanced care planning\n- Hospital diversion initiatives\n- AI-powered health risk analytics (fever, respiratory symptoms, antibiotic usage patterns)\n- Integration with WashSense smart handwashing devices for real-time hygiene compliance monitoring\n\nIt is unclear how much of the broader Arsana platform is part of the certified BroadStreet product vs. separate products in the same ecosystem.\n\n### Data & Content\n\n**Data types confirmed by certification criteria:**\n- Patient demographics\n- Medication orders and medication lists\n- Laboratory orders and results\n- Diagnostic imaging orders\n- Problem lists\n- Allergy lists\n- Family health history\n- Implantable device records\n- Social, psychological, and behavioral data (SDOH-adjacent)\n- Care plans\n- Clinical quality measure data (depression screening, BMI, tobacco use, fall risk, cognitive assessment, kidney health)\n- C-CDA documents (for transitions of care)\n- Patient-generated health data\n- Audit logs and access records\n\n**Data types implied by Arsana platform marketing (but not confirmed as part of BroadStreet specifically):**\n- Infection surveillance data\n- Hand hygiene compliance data\n- Cross-contamination mapping\n- Records aggregated from external sources (doctors' offices, labs, pharmacies)\n\n**What's unclear / not mentioned:**\n- **Billing and claims data:** Not mentioned anywhere. The certification criteria don't include billing-related items, and neither the vendor website nor any marketing materials mention billing functionality. The Arsana/CORI press release mentions the platform \"creates new billing opportunities\" but doesn't describe integrated billing/claims modules. Billing is likely handled by a separate system.\n- **Scheduling:** Not mentioned.\n- **Progress notes / clinical narrative documentation:** Not explicitly described, though likely present given the clinical charting implied by CPOE and CQM capabilities.\n- **Nursing assessments (MDS, OASIS, etc.):** Not mentioned, despite the post-acute focus where MDS assessments are a core workflow for SNFs. This is a notable gap in available information.\n- **eMAR (electronic medication administration record):** Not mentioned, though this would be expected for a post-acute EHR.\n- **Pharmacy integration:** Not explicitly described.\n- **Messaging/communication between staff:** Not mentioned.\n\nThe overall picture is of an early-stage post-acute EHR with a solid set of ONC-certified clinical capabilities, but with very limited publicly available information about the full breadth of the product's functionality. The vendor website and marketing materials are sparse, making it difficult to assess what the product does beyond what's required for certification.\n","numLines":125,"startLine":1,"totalLines":125}}}

data-svelte-h=\"svelte-pen992\">Real World Testing</h2> <h3 class=\"text-[1.2em]\" data-svelte-h=\"svelte-tywj49\">About The ONC Real World Testing Plan</h3> <p data-svelte-h=\"svelte-fd0hc1\">Real World Testing is a process by which Health IT Developers demonstrate interoperability and functionality of their Certified Health IT in real world settings and scenarios, rather than in a controlled test environment with an ONC-Authorized Testing Lab (ONC-ATL). Real World Testing verifies that deployed Certified Health IT continues to perform as intended by conducting and measuring observations of interoperability and data exchange. data-svelte-h=\"svelte-pen992\">Real World Testing</h2> <h3 class=\"text-[1.2em]\" data-svelte-h=\"svelte-tywj49\">About The ONC Real World Testing Plan</h3> <p data-svelte-h=\"svelte-fd0hc1\">Real World Testing is a process by which Health IT Developers demonstrate interoperability and functionality of their Certified Health IT in real world settings and scenarios, rather than in a controlled test environment with an ONC-Authorized Testing Lab (ONC-ATL). Real World Testing verifies that deployed Certified Health IT continues to perform as intended by conducting and measuring observations of interoperability and data exchange. These observations are described in a public and transparent way through Real World Testing plans and reported as Real World Testing results.</p> <table class=\"table mb-16 overflow-hidden rounded-lg text-base text-center shadow-xl\" data-svelte-h=\"svelte-16bih8n\"><tr class=\"border-b\"><th>Year</th> <th>Version</th> <th>Plan</th> <th>Report</th></tr> <tr class=\"border-b svelte-l87py\"><td class=\"svelte-l87py\">2025</td> <td class=\"svelte-l87py\">1</td> <td class=\"svelte-l87py\"><a href=\"/documents/BST%202025%20RWT%20Plan%20SLI%20ONC_Final.pdf\">2025 RWT Plan</a></td> <td class=\"svelte-l87py\"></td></tr></table> <p class=\"mt-12\" data-svelte-h=\"svelte-n6g0jx\">ONC CERTIFIED HIT® is a registered trademark of HHS.</p></div> </div></div></main> <footer class=\"p-6 text-gray-500 border-t-gray-200 border-t border-solid bt-1\" data-svelte-h=\"svelte-1lzjsmf\"><div class=\"container\">© 2024 BroadStreet Health, LLC - All Rights Reserved</div></footer></div> \n\t\t\t\n\t\t\t<script>\n\t\t\t\t{\n\t\t\t\t\t__sveltekit_1hrlrun = {\n\t\t\t\t\t\tbase: \"\",\n\t\t\t\t\t\tenv: {}\n\t\t\t\t\t};\n\n\t\t\t\t\tconst element = document.currentScript.parentElement;\n\n\t\t\t\t\tconst data = [null,null,null,null];\n\n\t\t\t\t\tPromise.all([\n\t\t\t\t\t\timport(\"/_app/immutable/entry/start.d6698d69.js\"),\n\t\t\t\t\t\timport(\"/_app/immutable/entry/app.098e4f55.js\")\n\t\t\t\t\t]).then(([kit, app]) => {\n\t\t\t\t\t\tkit.start(app, element, {\n\t\t\t\t\t\t\tnode_ids: [0, 2, 4, 7],\n\t\t\t\t\t\t\tdata,\n\t\t\t\t\t\t\tform: null,\n\t\t\t\t\t\t\terror: null\n\t\t\t\t\t\t});\n\t\t\t\t\t});\n\t\t\t\t}\n\t\t\t</script>\n\t\t</div>\n    </body>\n</html>\n","numLines":63,"startLine":1,"totalLines":63}}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01Qyxq66wi8frFzgxD3XqdR9","type":"tool_result","content":[{"type":"text","text":"# evaluate_script response\nScript ran on page and returned:\n```json\n\"<h1>EHI Export</h1> <p>BroadStreet Health exports EHI in formats defined by the CDA 2.1 and FHIR R4 standards. BroadStreet Health ensures that our exported electronic health information is both comprehensive and interoperable, facilitating seamless data sharing across the healthcare spectrum.</p> <h2 id=\\\"clinical-document-architecture-cda-21\\\"><a href=\\\"#clinical-document-architecture-cda-21\\\" aria-hidden=\\\"true\\\" tabindex=\\\"-1\\\"><span class=\\\"icon icon-link\\\"></span></a>Clinical Document Architecture (CDA) 2.1</h2> <p><strong>Overview:</strong> CDA, or Clinical Document Architecture, is a standard developed by Health Level Seven International (HL7) to ensure uniformity in the structure of electronic clinical documents. CDA 2.1 represents one of the more recent versions of this standard and is widely used in health IT systems.</p> <p><strong>Key Features of CDA 2.1:</strong></p> <ul><li><strong>Structured Documents:</strong> CDA ensures that all clinical documents have a consistent structure, including a header and a body.</li> <li><strong>Human Readability:</strong> Every CDA document is human-readable, meaning that in its most basic form, it can be displayed and understood by clinicians.</li> <li><strong>Machine Processability:</strong> Beyond the human-readable component, additional structured data can be added, making the document machine-processable.</li></ul> <p><strong>Structure:</strong></p> <ul><li><strong>Header:</strong> Contains metadata about the document itself such as the patient, author, and type of document.</li> <li><strong>Body:</strong> Houses the clinical information and is divided into sections. Each section can have one or multiple entries detailing the clinical act, such as a diagnosis, observation, or medication.</li></ul> <p><strong>Usage in BroadStreet Health:</strong> Our system exports patient data in the form of CDA documents that adhere to the CDA 2.1 standard. These documents can be seamlessly shared and integrated into other EHR systems or healthcare IT systems supporting the CDA standard.</p> <p>For a more in-depth understanding and the specification of CDA 2.1, please refer to the official <a href=\\\"https://www.hl7.org/implement/standards/product_brief.cfm?product_id=7\\\" rel=\\\"nofollow\\\">HL7 CDA documentation</a>.</p> <h2 id=\\\"fast-healthcare-interoperability-resources-fhir-r4\\\"><a href=\\\"#fast-healthcare-interoperability-resources-fhir-r4\\\" aria-hidden=\\\"true\\\" tabindex=\\\"-1\\\"><span class=\\\"icon icon-link\\\"></span></a>Fast Healthcare Interoperability Resources (FHIR) R4</h2> <p><strong>Overview:</strong> FHIR (pronounced “fire”) is another standard developed by HL7, focusing on the interoperability of healthcare information. Unlike the document-centric approach of CDA, FHIR is resource-oriented, with each resource representing a different type of clinical information.</p> <p><strong>Key Features of FHIR R4:</strong></p> <ul><li><strong>Granular Data:</strong> Instead of whole documents, FHIR deals with individual pieces of clinical information, like a patient’s demographics, an observation, or a medication order.</li> <li><strong>Web Standards:</strong> FHIR is built upon modern web standards, making it easier for developers to work with.</li> <li><strong>Extensibility:</strong> FHIR resources can be extended or adapted for specific use-cases or to support local workflows.</li></ul> <p><strong>Resources:</strong> FHIR defines a set of resources for different clinical concepts. Amongst other, it includes resources, such as:</p> <ul><li><strong>Patient:</strong> Information about an individual receiving care.</li> <li><strong>Observation:</strong> Measurements or simple assertions made about a patient.</li> <li><strong>Medication:</strong> Details about a medication that can be prescribed.</li> <li><strong>Practitioner:</strong> An individual with a formal responsibility in the healthcare process. Examples include doctors, nurses, and pharmacists.</li> <li><strong>Encounter:</strong> An interaction between a patient and the healthcare provider, such as a visit or an inpatient admission.</li> <li><strong>Procedure:</strong> A clinical activity or intervention performed on or for a patient, like surgery or a diagnostic test.</li> <li><strong>Condition:</strong> A clinical condition, problem, or diagnosis about a patient.</li> <li><strong>Immunization:</strong> A record of an immunization that has been given to a patient.</li> <li><strong>AllergyIntolerance:</strong> Details about an adverse reaction or allergy a patient has to substances, including medications.</li> <li><strong>MedicationRequest:</strong> A request for a medication to be administered or dispensed to a patient.</li> <li><strong>CarePlan:</strong> A plan or protocol established to manage a patient’s specific health concerns.</li> <li><strong>Device:</strong> Details about a medical device that is used on or for a patient, such as a pacemaker or an insulin pump.</li> <li><strong>DiagnosticReport:</strong> The findings and interpretation of diagnostic tests performed on patients.</li> <li><strong>Appointment:</strong> A scheduled interaction between a patient and a healthcare provider.</li> <li><strong>Organization:</strong> Represents an organization, such as a hospital or clinic, involved in the care of a patient.</li></ul> <p><strong>Usage in BroadStreet Health:</strong> When exporting data in FHIR format, our system provides a bundle of FHIR resources. Each resource within the bundle contains a segment of the patient’s health data. evaluate_script response\nScript ran on page and returned:\n```json\n\"<h1>EHI Export</h1> <p>BroadStreet Health exports EHI in formats defined by the CDA 2.1 and FHIR R4 standards. BroadStreet Health ensures that our exported electronic health information is both comprehensive and interoperable, facilitating seamless data sharing across the healthcare spectrum.</p> <h2 id=\\\"clinical-document-architecture-cda-21\\\"><a href=\\\"#clinical-document-architecture-cda-21\\\" aria-hidden=\\\"true\\\" tabindex=\\\"-1\\\"><span class=\\\"icon icon-link\\\"></span></a>Clinical Document Architecture (CDA) 2.1</h2> <p><strong>Overview:</strong> CDA, or Clinical Document Architecture, is a standard developed by Health Level Seven International (HL7) to ensure uniformity in the structure of electronic clinical documents. CDA 2.1 represents one of the more recent versions of this standard and is widely used in health IT systems.</p> <p><strong>Key Features of CDA 2.1:</strong></p> <ul><li><strong>Structured Documents:</strong> CDA ensures that all clinical documents have a consistent structure, including a header and a body.</li> <li><strong>Human Readability:</strong> Every CDA document is human-readable, meaning that in its most basic form, it can be displayed and understood by clinicians.</li> <li><strong>Machine Processability:</strong> Beyond the human-readable component, additional structured data can be added, making the document machine-processable.</li></ul> <p><strong>Structure:</strong></p> <ul><li><strong>Header:</strong> Contains metadata about the document itself such as the patient, author, and type of document.</li> <li><strong>Body:</strong> Houses the clinical information and is divided into sections. Each section can have one or multiple entries detailing the clinical act, such as a diagnosis, observation, or medication.</li></ul> <p><strong>Usage in BroadStreet Health:</strong> Our system exports patient data in the form of CDA documents that adhere to the CDA 2.1 standard. These documents can be seamlessly shared and integrated into other EHR systems or healthcare IT systems supporting the CDA standard.</p> <p>For a more in-depth understanding and the specification of CDA 2.1, please refer to the official <a href=\\\"https://www.hl7.org/implement/standards/product_brief.cfm?product_id=7\\\" rel=\\\"nofollow\\\">HL7 CDA documentation</a>.</p> <h2 id=\\\"fast-healthcare-interoperability-resources-fhir-r4\\\"><a href=\\\"#fast-healthcare-interoperability-resources-fhir-r4\\\" aria-hidden=\\\"true\\\" tabindex=\\\"-1\\\"><span class=\\\"icon icon-link\\\"></span></a>Fast Healthcare Interoperability Resources (FHIR) R4</h2> <p><strong>Overview:</strong> FHIR (pronounced “fire”) is another standard developed by HL7, focusing on the interoperability of healthcare information. Unlike the document-centric approach of CDA, FHIR is resource-oriented, with each resource representing a different type of clinical information.</p> <p><strong>Key Features of FHIR R4:</strong></p> <ul><li><strong>Granular Data:</strong> Instead of whole documents, FHIR deals with individual pieces of clinical information, like a patient’s demographics, an observation, or a medication order.</li> <li><strong>Web Standards:</strong> FHIR is built upon modern web standards, making it easier for developers to work with.</li> <li><strong>Extensibility:</strong> FHIR resources can be extended or adapted for specific use-cases or to support local workflows.</li></ul> <p><strong>Resources:</strong> FHIR defines a set of resources for different clinical concepts. Amongst other, it includes resources, such as:</p> <ul><li><strong>Patient:</strong> Information about an individual receiving care.</li> <li><strong>Observation:</strong> Measurements or simple assertions made about a patient.</li> <li><strong>Medication:</strong> Details about a medication that can be prescribed.</li> <li><strong>Practitioner:</strong> An individual with a formal responsibility in the healthcare process. Examples include doctors, nurses, and pharmacists.</li> <li><strong>Encounter:</strong> An interaction between a patient and the healthcare provider, such as a visit or an inpatient admission.</li> <li><strong>Procedure:</strong> A clinical activity or intervention performed on or for a patient, like surgery or a diagnostic test.</li> <li><strong>Condition:</strong> A clinical condition, problem, or diagnosis about a patient.</li> <li><strong>Immunization:</strong> A record of an immunization that has been given to a patient.</li> <li><strong>AllergyIntolerance:</strong> Details about an adverse reaction or allergy a patient has to substances, including medications.</li> <li><strong>MedicationRequest:</strong> A request for a medication to be administered or dispensed to a patient.</li> <li><strong>CarePlan:</strong> A plan or protocol established to manage a patient’s specific health concerns.</li> <li><strong>Device:</strong> Details about a medical device that is used on or for a patient, such as a pacemaker or an insulin pump.</li> <li><strong>DiagnosticReport:</strong> The findings and interpretation of diagnostic tests performed on patients.</li> <li><strong>Appointment:</strong> A scheduled interaction between a patient and a healthcare provider.</li> <li><strong>Organization:</strong> Represents an organization, such as a hospital or clinic, involved in the care of a patient.</li></ul> <p><strong>Usage in BroadStreet Health:</strong> When exporting data in FHIR format, our system provides a bundle of FHIR resources. Each resource within the bundle contains a segment of the patient’s health data. CDA 2.1 represents one of the more recent versions of this standard and is widely used in health IT systems.</p> <p><strong>Key Features of CDA 2.1:</strong></p> <ul><li><strong>Structured Documents:</strong> CDA ensures that all clinical documents have a consistent structure, including a header and a body.</li> <li><strong>Human Readability:</strong> Every CDA document is human-readable, meaning that in its most basic form, it can be displayed and understood by clinicians.</li> <li><strong>Machine Processability:</strong> Beyond the human-readable component, additional structured data can be added, making the document machine-processable.</li></ul> <p><strong>Structure:</strong></p> <ul><li><strong>Header:</strong> Contains metadata about the document itself such as the patient, author, and type of document.</li> <li><strong>Body:</strong> Houses the clinical information and is divided into sections. Each section can have one or multiple entries detailing the clinical act, such as a diagnosis, observation, or medication.</li></ul> <p><strong>Usage in BroadStreet Health:</strong> Our system exports patient data in the form of CDA documents that adhere to the CDA 2.1 standard. These documents can be seamlessly shared and integrated into other EHR systems or healthcare IT systems supporting the CDA standard.</p> <p>For a more in-depth understanding and the specification of CDA 2.1, please refer to the official <a href=\\\"https://www.hl7.org/implement/standards/product_brief.cfm?product_id=7\\\" rel=\\\"nofollow\\\">HL7 CDA documentation</a>.</p> <h2 id=\\\"fast-healthcare-interoperability-resources-fhir-r4\\\"><a href=\\\"#fast-healthcare-interoperability-resources-fhir-r4\\\" aria-hidden=\\\"true\\\" tabindex=\\\"-1\\\"><span class=\\\"icon icon-link\\\"></span></a>Fast Healthcare Interoperability Resources (FHIR) R4</h2> <p><strong>Overview:</strong> FHIR (pronounced “fire”) is another standard developed by HL7, focusing on the interoperability of healthcare information. Unlike the document-centric approach of CDA, FHIR is resource-oriented, with each resource representing a different type of clinical information.</p> <p><strong>Key Features of FHIR R4:</strong></p> <ul><li><strong>Granular Data:</strong> Instead of whole documents, FHIR deals with individual pieces of clinical information, like a patient’s demographics, an observation, or a medication order.</li> <li><strong>Web Standards:</strong> FHIR is built upon modern web standards, making it easier for developers to work with.</li> <li><strong>Extensibility:</strong> FHIR resources can be extended or adapted for specific use-cases or to support local workflows.</li></ul> <p><strong>Resources:</strong> FHIR defines a set of resources for different clinical concepts. Amongst other, it includes resources, such as:</p> <ul><li><strong>Patient:</strong> Information about an individual receiving care.</li> <li><strong>Observation:</strong> Measurements or simple assertions made about a patient.</li> <li><strong>Medication:</strong> Details about a medication that can be prescribed.</li> <li><strong>Practitioner:</strong> An individual with a formal responsibility in the healthcare process. Examples include doctors, nurses, and pharmacists.</li> <li><strong>Encounter:</strong> An interaction between a patient and the healthcare provider, such as a visit or an inpatient admission.</li> <li><strong>Procedure:</strong> A clinical activity or intervention performed on or for a patient, like surgery or a diagnostic test.</li> <li><strong>Condition:</strong> A clinical condition, problem, or diagnosis about a patient.</li> <li><strong>Immunization:</strong> A record of an immunization that has been given to a patient.</li> <li><strong>AllergyIntolerance:</strong> Details about an adverse reaction or allergy a patient has to substances, including medications.</li> <li><strong>MedicationRequest:</strong> A request for a medication to be administered or dispensed to a patient.</li> <li><strong>CarePlan:</strong> A plan or protocol established to manage a patient’s specific health concerns.</li> <li><strong>Device:</strong> Details about a medical device that is used on or for a patient, such as a pacemaker or an insulin pump.</li> <li><strong>DiagnosticReport:</strong> The findings and interpretation of diagnostic tests performed on patients.</li> <li><strong>Appointment:</strong> A scheduled interaction between a patient and a healthcare provider.</li> <li><strong>Organization:</strong> Represents an organization, such as a hospital or clinic, involved in the care of a patient.</li></ul> <p><strong>Usage in BroadStreet Health:</strong> When exporting data in FHIR format, our system provides a bundle of FHIR resources. Each resource within the bundle contains a segment of the patient’s health data. This granular approach ensures that data can be easily integrated, searched, and combined with data from other sources.</p> <p>For more comprehensive details and the specification of FHIR R4, please BroadStreet Health ensures that our exported electronic health information is both comprehensive and interoperable, facilitating seamless data sharing across the healthcare spectrum.</p> <h2 id=\"clinical-document-architecture-cda-21\"><a href=\"#clinical-document-architecture-cda-21\" aria-hidden=\"true\" tabindex=\"-1\"><span class=\"icon icon-link\"></span></a>Clinical Document Architecture (CDA) 2.1</h2> <p><strong>Overview:</strong> CDA, or Clinical Document Architecture, is a standard developed by Health Level Seven International (HL7) to ensure uniformity in the structure of electronic clinical documents. CDA 2.1 represents one of the more recent versions of this standard and is widely used in health IT systems.</p> <p><strong>Key Features of CDA 2.1:</strong></p> <ul><li><strong>Structured Documents:</strong> CDA ensures that all clinical documents have a consistent structure, including a header and a body.</li> <li><strong>Human Readability:</strong> Every CDA document is human-readable, meaning that in its most basic form, it can be displayed and understood by clinicians.</li> <li><strong>Machine Processability:</strong> Beyond the human-readable component, additional structured data can be added, making the document machine-processable.</li></ul> <p><strong>Structure:</strong></p> <ul><li><strong>Header:</strong> Contains metadata about the document itself such as the patient, author, and type of document.</li> <li><strong>Body:</strong> Houses the clinical information and is divided into sections. Each section can have one or multiple entries detailing the clinical act, such as a diagnosis, observation, or medication.</li></ul> <p><strong>Usage in BroadStreet Health:</strong> Our system exports patient data in the form of CDA documents that adhere to the CDA 2.1 standard. These documents can be seamlessly shared and integrated into other EHR systems or healthcare IT systems supporting the CDA standard.</p> <p>For a more in-depth understanding and the specification of CDA 2.1, please refer to the official <a href=\"https://www.hl7.org/implement/standards/product_brief.cfm?product_id=7\" rel=\"nofollow\">HL7 CDA documentation</a>.</p> <h2 id=\"fast-healthcare-interoperability-resources-fhir-r4\"><a href=\"#fast-healthcare-interoperability-resources-fhir-r4\" aria-hidden=\"true\" tabindex=\"-1\"><span class=\"icon icon-link\"></span></a>Fast Healthcare Interoperability Resources (FHIR) R4</h2> <p><strong>Overview:</strong> FHIR (pronounced &ldquo;fire&rdquo;) is another standard developed by HL7, focusing on the interoperability of healthcare information. Unlike the document-centric approach of CDA, FHIR is resource-oriented, with each resource representing a different type of clinical information.</p> <p><strong>Key Features of FHIR R4:</strong></p> <ul><li><strong>Granular Data:</strong> Instead of whole documents, FHIR deals with individual pieces of clinical information, like a patient's demographics, an observation, or a medication order.</li> <li><strong>Web Standards:</strong> FHIR is built upon modern web standards, making it easier for developers to work with.</li> <li><strong>Extensibility:</strong> FHIR resources can be extended or adapted for specific use-cases or to support local workflows.</li></ul> <p><strong>Resources:</strong> FHIR defines a set of resources for different clinical concepts. Amongst other, it includes resources, such as:</p> <ul><li><strong>Patient:</strong> Information about an individual receiving care.</li> <li><strong>Observation:</strong> Measurements or simple assertions made about a patient.</li> <li><strong>Medication:</strong> Details about a medication that can be prescribed.</li> <li><strong>Practitioner:</strong> An individual with a formal responsibility in the healthcare process. Examples include doctors, nurses, and pharmacists.</li> <li><strong>Encounter:</strong> An interaction between a patient and the healthcare provider, such as a visit or an inpatient admission.</li> <li><strong>Procedure:</strong> A clinical activity or intervention performed on or for a patient, like surgery or a diagnostic test.</li> <li><strong>Condition:</strong> A clinical condition, problem, or diagnosis about a patient.</li> <li><strong>Immunization:</strong> A record of an immunization that has been given to a patient.</li> <li><strong>AllergyIntolerance:</strong> Details about an adverse reaction or allergy a patient has to substances, including medications.</li> <li><strong>MedicationRequest:</strong> A request for a medication to be administered or dispensed to a patient.</li> <li><strong>CarePlan:</strong> A plan or protocol established to manage a patient's specific health concerns.</li> <li><strong>Device:</strong> Details about a medical device that is used on or for a patient, such as a pacemaker or an insulin pump.</li> <li><strong>DiagnosticReport:</strong> The findings and interpretation of diagnostic tests performed on patients.</li> <li><strong>Appointment:</strong> A scheduled interaction between a patient and a healthcare provider.</li> <li><strong>Organization:</strong> Represents an organization, such as a hospital or clinic, involved in the care of a patient.</li></ul> <p><strong>Usage in BroadStreet Health:</strong> When exporting data in FHIR format, our system provides a bundle of FHIR resources. Each resource within the bundle contains a segment of the patient's health data. This granular approach ensures that data can be easily integrated, searched, and combined with data from other sources.</p> <p>For more comprehensive details and the specification of FHIR R4, please refer to the official <a href=\"https://hl7.org/fhir/R4B/overview.html\" rel=\"nofollow\">HL7 FHIR R4 documentation</a>.</p> <h2 id=\"broadstreet-notes-html\"><a href=\"#broadstreet-notes-html\" aria-hidden=\"true\" tabindex=\"-1\"><span class=\"icon icon-link\"></span></a>Broadstreet Notes (HTML)</h2> <p><strong>Overview</strong> Notes created in Broadstreet can be exported in HTML format with the following structure.</p> <h3 id=\"physician-information\"><a href=\"#physician-information\" aria-hidden=\"true\" tabindex=\"-1\"><span class=\"icon icon-link\"></span></a>Physician Information</h3> <ul><li><strong>Physician Name</strong>: The name of the physician who provided the service.</li> <li><strong>Sent by</strong>: The individual who sent the documentation.</li> <li><strong>Date</strong>: The timestamp when the documentation was sent.</li></ul> <h3 id=\"visit-and-patient-details\"><a href=\"#visit-and-patient-details\" aria-hidden=\"true\" tabindex=\"-1\"><span class=\"icon icon-link\"></span></a>Visit and Patient Details</h3> <ul><li><strong>Date of Service</strong>: The date when the service was provided.</li> Birth</strong>: The birthdate of the patient.</li> <li><strong>Gender</strong>: The gender of the patient.</li> <li><strong>Advance Directive Code</strong>: Any code related to advance directives for CDA 2.1 (Clinical Document Architecture)\nA general description of the CDA 2.1 standard — structured documents with headers (patient, author, document type metadata) and bodies (clinical sections with entries for diagnoses, observations, medications). The page states: \"Our system exports patient data in the form of CDA documents that adhere to the CDA 2.1 standard.\" Links to external HL7 CDA documentation. No BroadStreet-specific CDA template, profile, or section list is provided.\n\n### 2. FHIR R4\nA general description of the FHIR R4 standard with a list of resource types: Patient, Observation, Medication, Practitioner, Encounter, Procedure, Condition, Immunization, AllergyIntolerance, MedicationRequest, CarePlan, Device, DiagnosticReport, Appointment, Organization. The page states: \"When exporting data in FHIR format, our system provides a bundle of FHIR resources.\" Links to external HL7 FHIR R4 documentation. This is clearly (g)(10) standardized API documentation, not (b)(10) bulk export documentation.\n\n### 3. BroadStreet Notes (HTML)\nThe most specific section: describes the structure of clinical notes exported as HTML. Fields documented:\n- **Physician Information:** Physician Name, Sent by, Date\n- **Visit and Patient Details:** Date of Service, Type (e.g., H&P), Patient Name, Date of Birth, Gender, Advance Directive Code\n- **Medical Concerns:** Allergies, Primary Concern\n- **Social History:** Smoking Status\n- **Treatment Review:** (described as \"typically include past and ongoing treatments\")\n- **Vital Examination:** Blood Pressure, Pulse, Weight\n- **Assessment Plan:** Diagnosis Code (ICD), Assessment\n- **Visit Details:** Next Appointment, Reason for Next Visit\n- **Additional Notes:** Physician remarks\n- **Signature:** Electronically Signed By\n\nThis is the only BroadStreet-specific data structure documentation on the page.\n\n### RWT Plan (b)(10) Test Case\nThe 2025 Real World Testing Plan includes Test Case 4 for (b)(10), which describes:\n- Testing in a production environment at an assisted living facility\n- Tracking frequency and completion times of export requests via logs\n- Both individual and population-level EHI exports\n- Compatibility verification by importing exported data into external systems\n- Expected outcome: \"consistently generate and export EHI for both individual patients and populations, ensuring the data is complete, accurate, and properly formatted\"\n\nThe RWT Plan mentions the system supports \"multiple specialties across Post Acute, Long Term Care, and community-based settings.\"\n\n## Export Coverage Assessment\n\n### Data Domain Coverage\n\nThe EHI export documentation is remarkably thin on specifics about what data domains are actually included in the export. Here is the assessment against what's known about the product:\n\n**Domains apparently covered (based on FHIR resource list and Notes structure):**\n- Patient demographics\n- Conditions/problems\n- Medications (Medication, MedicationRequest)\n- Allergies (AllergyIntolerance)\n- Immunizations\n- Observations (vitals, labs, social history, SDOH)\n- Procedures\n- Encounters\n- Care plans and goals\n- Care teams\n- Diagnostic reports\n- Devices (implantable)\n- Clinical notes (via HTML export)\n- Documents (DocumentReference)\n- Appointments\n- Service requests (orders)\n- Questionnaire responses\n\n**Domains with no mention or documentation:**\n- **Billing and claims data** — completely absent. No mention of charges, payments, claims, or billing codes anywhere in the export documentation. The product research suggests billing may be handled externally, but this is not clarified.\n- **Medication administration records (eMAR)** — not mentioned, despite being critical for post-acute care settings where medication administration tracking is a core workflow.\n- **Nursing assessments (MDS, OASIS)** — not documented, despite being fundamental to the post-acute care workflow. MDS assessments are mandatory for SNF residents.\n- **Family health history** — certified for (a)(12) but not listed in FHIR resources or EHI export documentation.\n- **Clinical quality measure data** — certified for CQMs but no mention in the export.\n- **Scheduling data** — Appointment is listed on the EHI export page's FHIR resources list but not in the actual FHIR API resources page, suggesting inconsistency.\n\n### Export Format & Standards\n\nThe export uses three formats:\n1. **CDA 2.1** — a recognized standard, but no BroadStreet-specific template or section list is provided. It's impossible to tell which CDA sections are populated or how complete the document is.\n2. **FHIR R4** — a recognized standard using US Core STU 5.0.1 profiles. The resource list is essentially the standard US Core set. This strongly suggests the FHIR export is the same as the (g)(10) API — covering USCDI data classes, not all EHI.\n3. **HTML Notes** — a proprietary format for clinical notes with a defined field structure.\n\n**The (b)(10) vs (g)(10) confusion is present here.** The FHIR resource list on the EHI export page maps closely to the standard US Core resource set. The separate FHIR API documentation confirms these are the same resources served by the (g)(10) endpoint at `portal.broadstreetcare.com/api/fhir/`. The documentation does not describe how the EHI export goes beyond what the (g)(10) API provides. There is no mention of exporting data that doesn't fit into FHIR US Core — no billing data, no specialty-specific post-acute care assessments, no custom data structures.\n\nThe CDA export likely produces C-CDA documents (given the (b)(1) Transitions of Care certification and SVAP update to C-CDA R2.1 Companion Guide Release 3), which would cover a similar scope to the FHIR resources — standard clinical summaries, not comprehensive EHI.\n\n### Documentation Quality\n\n**Very poor.** The EHI export page is largely a textbook description of the CDA and FHIR standards themselves, not documentation of BroadStreet's specific implementation. Key deficiencies:\n\n- **No data dictionary** — no table/field definitions, no schema, no column listings\n- **No export format specification** — no description of the actual file structure, bundle format, or packaging of the export\n- **No export instructions** — no user guide for performing an export (how to trigger it, what options are available, what files are produced)\n- **No sample data or examples** — no example export files, sample records, or test data\n- **No machine-readable artifacts** — no JSON Schema, XSD, OpenAPI spec, or any other computable format description\n- **No value set or coded field documentation** — coded fields like diagnosis codes are mentioned but value sets are not specified\n- **No relationship documentation** — how entities relate to each other in the export is not described\n\nThe BroadStreet Notes (HTML) section is the only part that documents BroadStreet-specific data structure, and even that is a simple field list without data types, constraints, or examples.\n\nThe FHIR resource documentation in the separate `/docs/fhir/` section is more structured (with search parameters, profiles, and endpoints), but this is (g)(10) API documentation, not (b)(10) export documentation.\n\n### Structure & Completeness\n\n- **Granularity:** The EHI export page provides resource-level descriptions only (e.g., \"Patient: Information about an individual receiving care\"). No field-level detail for the FHIR or CDA formats. The Notes HTML section provides field-level names but no data types or constraints.\n- **Coded fields:** Diagnosis Code is described as \"ICD code\" — no further specification of code systems, value sets, or terminology mappings.\n- **Relationships:** Not documented at all.\n- **Versioning:** No version history or change tracking.\n\n## Overall Assessment\n\nBroadStreet's EHI export documentation reads as a minimal compliance checkbox rather than a genuine attempt to document how their system exports all electronic health information.
